Trapped surface formation for the Einstein-Weyl spinor system
Peng Zhao, Xiaoning Wu

Abstract
We prove trapped-surface formation for the Einstein-Weyl spinor system (gravity coupled to a massless left-handed two-spinor) without any symmetry assumption. To this end we establish a semi-global solution under double null foliation and show that the focusing of the gravitational waves and the Weyl spinor flux leads to the formation of a trapped surface.



1
Introduction

Following Penrose’s singularity theorem, the presence of a trapped surface-a closed
spacelike 2-sphere on which both future-directed null expansions are negative-leads
to the incompleteness of future causal geodesics.
In practice this is taken as rigorous evidence for a black hole.
Consequently, the dynamical formation of a black hole can be framed-mathematically
and conceptually-as the problem of producing a trapped surface from physically meaningful initial data.
There is by now a substantial body of work on trapped-surface formation
in vacuum, including foundational results of Christodoulou
[
1
]
, and continued in

[
2
,
4
,
5
,
7
,
8
]

and more recent criteria in matter models (e.g. electromagnetic, scalar, and Yang-Mills fields) in

[
3
,
6
,
9
,
10
,
15
,
16
]
.
For recent nonlinear numerical simulations of black-hole formation and related dynamics, see, e.g.,

[
17
,
18
,
19
,
20
]
.

In this work, we study the Einstein equations minimally coupled to a left-handed Weyl spinor;
see Sec.
1.1
.
Weyl spinors model massless, chiral spin-
1/2
degrees of freedom that appear ubiquitously in the Standard Model.
Understanding their dynamics under strong gravity provides a baseline
for gravitational collapse of fermionic, e.g. neutron, proton, electron and so on.
On the small-data, far-from-collapse side, Chen
[
21
]

has established the global nonlinear stability of Minkowski space for
the massless Einstein-Dirac system in generalized wave gauge via the vector-field method,
providing a robust baseline for the diffusion dynamics of spinor fields coupled to gravity.
On the static/spherically symmetric side,
Finster-Smoller-Yau
[
22
]
proved a no-hair type result for the spherically symmetric,
static Einstein-Dirac-Maxwell system: under mild regularity of the horizon,
the only black-hole solutions have vanishing spinors in the exterior region and equivalently,
reduce to Reissner-Nordström.
Beyond Finster-Smoller-Yau, stationary nonexistence results now cover charged, rotating (A)dS geometries:
Wang-Zhang
[
23
]
rule out time-periodic Dirac fields in non-extreme Kerr-Newman-AdS,
while Zhang-Zhang
[
24
]
exclude differentiable time-periodic Majorana fermions
in Kerr-Newman[-(A)dS] with nonzero charge.
Numerically, nonlinear simulations by
Ventrella-Choptuik
[
25
]
for the massless Einstein-Dirac system
display Type II critical collapse with a characteristic mass-scaling exponent,
indicating that spinor flux can trigger black-hole formation at threshold.
To the best of our knowledge, however, a symmetry-free, characteristic-data theory
that proves trapped-surface formation for Dirac/Weyl spinors-on
par with vacuum and classical matter cases—has not yet been established.
This gap motivates our present analysis of trapped-surface formation in the Einstein-Weyl spinor system.

Conventions.

In this article, lowercase Latin
a,b,c,\dots
denote abstract tensor indices,
while
{\bm{a}},{\bm{b}},{\bm{c}},\dots
are tetrad indices taking values
0,\dots,3
;
uppercase Latin
A,B,C,\dots
are abstract two–spinor indices,
and
{\bm{A}},{\bm{B}},{\bm{C}},\dots
are spin–frame indices taking values
0,1
.
The alternating spinor
\epsilon_{AB}
gives the pairing

\llbracket\xi,\eta\rrbracket:=\epsilon_{AB}\,\xi^{A}\eta^{B}
;
indices are moved with
\epsilon^{AB}
and
\epsilon_{AB}
, e.g.

\xi_{B}=\epsilon_{AB}\,\xi^{A}
. For a normalized spin dyad
\{o^{A},\iota^{A}\}
with
o_{A}\iota^{A}=1
,
we have
\epsilon_{AB}=o_{A}\iota_{B}-o_{B}\iota_{A}
, and we set
\epsilon_{\bm{0}}{}^{A}=o^{A}
,

\epsilon_{\bm{1}}{}^{A}=\iota^{A}
. We choose an orthonormal tetrad
e_{{\bm{a}}}

with dual
\omega^{{\bm{a}}}
so that
g_{{\bm{a}}{\bm{b}}}=\eta_{{\bm{a}}{\bm{b}}}
with signature
(+,-,-,-)
.
The Infeld–van der Waerden symbols
\sigma^{{\bm{a}}}{}_{{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}

relate vectors to bi-spinors via

\epsilon_{{\bm{A}}{\bm{B}}}\,\epsilon_{{\bm{A}}^{\prime}{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}=\eta_{{\bm{a}}{\bm{b}}}\,\sigma^{{\bm{a}}}{}_{{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}\,\sigma^{{\bm{b}}}{}_{{\bm{B}}{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}
;
here
\sqrt{2}\,\sigma^{{\bm{a}}}{}_{{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}
corresponds to
\{\mathbf{1},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\}

where
\sigma_{i}
is the Pauli matries,
and
\sigma_{{\bm{a}}}{}^{{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}
denotes the inverse
with
\sigma^{{\bm{a}}}{}_{{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}\sigma_{{\bm{a}}}{}^{{\bm{B}}{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\bm{A}}}{}^{{\bm{B}}}\epsilon_{{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}{}^{{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}
.
For a tensor
T_{a}{}^{b}
we define its spinorial representative
by
T_{{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}{}^{\ \ {\bm{B}}{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}:=T_{{\bm{a}}}{}^{{\bm{b}}}\,\sigma^{{\bm{a}}}{}_{{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}\,\sigma_{{\bm{b}}}{}^{{\bm{B}}{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}
,
giving the standard correspondence between
T_{a}{}^{b}
and
T_{A}{}^{B}
;
we also use
g_{AA^{\prime}BB^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{AB}\epsilon_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
.
Our curvature sign convention is
\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\omega_{c}-\nabla_{b}\nabla_{a}\omega_{c}=-R_{abc}{}^{d}\,\omega_{d}
.
Spinor conventions follow
[
11
,
12
,
14
]
.

1.1
Einstein-Weyl System

In the massless limit a Dirac spinor decomposes into two decoupled Weyl spinors of opposite chirality.
The present work retains only the left-handed component,
i.e. a single-chirality subsector of the massless Einstein–Dirac system.
For the massive Dirac field, we refer the reader to
[
26
]
.
In the following, we denote by
\phi_{A}
the left-handed Weyl spinor field.
The Einstein-Weyl spinor system is

\displaystyle R_{ab}-\frac{1}{2}Rg_{ab}=

\displaystyle T_{ab},

(1)

\displaystyle\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\phi^{A}=

\displaystyle 0,

(2)

where
\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}
is the spinorial counterpart of the covariant derivative,

T_{ab}
is the energy-momentum tensor and its spinorial counterpart
T_{ABA^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
is defined by

\displaystyle T_{ABA^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\equiv-2\mathrm{i}\left(\phi_{A}\nabla_{BB^{\prime}}\phi_{A^{\prime}}-\phi_{A^{\prime}}\nabla_{BB^{\prime}}\phi_{A}+\phi_{B}\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\phi_{B^{\prime}}-\phi_{B^{\prime}}\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\phi_{B}\right).

(3)

In order to analyse the derivative term in the energy-momentum tensor and close the bootstrap argument,
we follow the strategy in our previous paper
[
26
]
,
i.e. make the irreducible decomposition for
\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\phi_{B}
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\phi_{B}=\nabla_{(A|A^{\prime}|}\phi_{B)}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{AB}\nabla_{CA^{\prime}}\phi^{C},

then the equation of motion of the Weyl spinor leads to 0 for the second term on the right hand.
Hence one defines the symmetric part be a new variable

\displaystyle\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}\equiv\nabla_{(A|A^{\prime}|}\phi_{B)}.

(4)

Its equation can be obtained by first commutating the covariant derivative to
\phi_{A}
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\nabla_{BB^{\prime}}\phi_{C}-\nabla_{BB^{\prime}}\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\phi_{C}=\epsilon_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\Box_{AB}\phi_{C}+\epsilon_{AB}\Box_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\phi_{C}

where

\displaystyle\Box_{AB}\equiv\nabla_{Q^{\prime}(A}\nabla_{B)}^{\phantom{B)}Q^{\prime}},\quad\Box_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\equiv\nabla_{Q(A^{\prime}}\nabla_{B^{\prime})}^{\phantom{B^{\prime})}Q}

and

\displaystyle\Box_{AB}\phi_{C}=\Psi_{ABCD}\phi^{D}-2\Lambda\phi_{(A}\epsilon_{B)C}=\Psi_{ABCD}\phi^{D},\quad\Box_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\phi_{C}=\Phi_{CDA^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\phi^{D},

here
\Lambda=-\frac{R}{24}
, which vanishes in this model.

\Psi_{ABCD}
is the spinorial counterpart of the Weyl curvature,

\Phi_{CDA^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
is the spinorial counterpart of the trace-free Ricci tensor.
One then concludes that
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
satisfies the following

\displaystyle\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\zeta_{BCB^{\prime}}-\nabla_{BB^{\prime}}\zeta_{ACA^{\prime}}=\Psi_{DCAB}\epsilon_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\phi^{D}+\Phi_{DCA^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\epsilon_{AB}\phi^{D}

(5)

Then with the definition of
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
as well as the equation of motion for
\phi_{A}
,
the energy-momentum tensor takes the form

\displaystyle T_{ABA^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\equiv-2\mathrm{i}\left(\phi_{A}\bar{\zeta}_{B^{\prime}A^{\prime}B}-\phi_{A^{\prime}}\zeta_{BAB^{\prime}}+\phi_{B}\bar{\zeta}_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}A}-\phi_{B^{\prime}}\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}\right)

(6)

where
\bar{\zeta}_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}A}
is the conjugate of
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
.
The Einstein field equations (
1
)
can be expressed in the spinorial form

\displaystyle\Phi_{ABB^{\prime}A^{\prime}}=\mathrm{i}\left(\phi_{A}\zeta_{B^{\prime}A^{\prime}B}-\phi_{A^{\prime}}\zeta_{BAB^{\prime}}+\phi_{B}\zeta_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}A}-\phi_{B^{\prime}}\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}\right).

(7)

1.2
Main theorem

In this section we show the main results of this paper:

Theorem 1

(
Existence result
)
.

Given a positive number
\mathcal{I}
, there exists a sufficiently
large
a_{0}=a_{0}(\mathcal{I})
such that for
a\geq a_{0}\geq 0
and
initial data

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}\equiv\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{15}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}||\textit{þ}^{j}(|u_{\infty}|\mathcal{D})^{i}(\sigma,\phi_{0})||_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}_{u_{\infty},v})}\leq\mathcal{I}

along the outgoing initial null hypersurface
u=u_{\infty}
, and
Minkowskian initial data along ingoing initial null
hypersurface
v=0
.
Then the Einstein-Weyl spinor system admits a unique solution in

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}=\{(u,v)|u_{\infty}\leq u\leq-a/4,\ \ 0\leq v\leq 1\}.

Based on the existence theorem we have the following theorem on trapped-surface formation:

Theorem 2

(
Trapped surface formation
)
.

Given
\mathcal{I}
, there exists a sufficiently large

a=a(\mathcal{I})
such that for
a\geq a_{0}\geq 0
an initial data set
with

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}\equiv\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{15}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}||\textit{þ}^{j}(|u_{\infty}|\mathcal{D})^{i}(\sigma,\phi_{0})||_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}_{u_{\infty},v})}\leq\mathcal{I}

along the outgoing initial null hypersurface
u=u_{\infty}
and
Minkowskian initial data along the ingoing initial null hypersurface

v=0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left(|u_{\infty}|^{2}(\sigma\bar{\sigma}+2\mathrm{i}(\phi_{0}\textit{þ}\bar{\phi}_{0}-\bar{\phi}_{0}\textit{þ}\phi_{0}))|\right)(u_{\infty},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\geq a

holds uniformly for any point on the initial outgoing null
hypersurface
u=u_{\infty}
, it follows that
\mathcal{S}_{-a/4,1}
is
a trapped surface.

Figure 1:
Existence domain
\mathbb{D}
for the main theorem.
We pose a characteristic problem with Minkowskian data on the incoming null hypersurface
v=0

and suitable outgoing data on
u=u_{\infty}
in the asymptotic region. For an appropriate choice of
a
,
the top-vertex 2-surface
\mathcal{S}_{-a/4,1}
is a trapped surface.

Remark 1
.

We work under double null foliation
(u,v)
in the T-weight formalism; see Sec.
2
.
We make use of the Newman-Penrose frame
\{{\bm{l}},{\bm{n}},\bm{m},\bar{\bm{m}}\}
,

\sigma
denotes the shear of outgoing light rays

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}(\widehat{\nabla_{a}l_{b}})

\displaystyle\equiv 2|\sigma|

where
\Pi_{\mathcal{S}}
is the projection operator,

\widehat{\nabla_{a}l_{b}}
is the traceless part of
\nabla_{a}l_{b}
.

\phi_{0}
is the
o
-component of spinor field
\phi_{A}
,

\textit{þ}\phi_{0}
is the derivative along
{\bm{l}}
.
The term
2\mathrm{i}(\phi_{0}\textit{þ}\bar{\phi}_{0}-\bar{\phi}_{0}\textit{þ}\phi_{0})
is
\Phi_{00}

and represent the energy flux of spinor field.

1.3
Main difficulty

Our argument is energy-based in a double-null foliation using T-weight framework.
The point of the present analysis is to highlight that the spinor

\phi_{A}
and its symmetric first derivative
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}

admit evolution/constraint equations whose signature is exactly compatible with
the scale-invariant norms
L^{2}_{sc}
.
This norm, whose definitions can be found in Sec.
3
,
is designed to capture the peeling property by assigning each quantity a signature
s_{2}
.
The equations for
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
provide sharp,
scale-critical control of
\phi_{A}
in both the angular and null directions
and remain aligned with the curvature hierarchy,
allowing the Bianchi–matter couplings to be quantified and absorbed at the same critical scale.
This compatibility allows us track the
u
-weight and obtain a scale-critical result by the bootstrap,
yielding closure without derivative loss for a quasilinear geometric system
whose stress–energy contains derivative–field products.

In the Bianchi sector coupled to the Weyl spinor,
large borderline contributions involving
\zeta_{4}
and
\zeta_{5}
(see Eq.
13
)
arise at every derivative order once one commutes with angular derivatives.
That is because pair
(\zeta_{4},\zeta_{5})
satisfies

\displaystyle\textit{þ}^{\prime}\zeta_{4}-\textit{ð}\zeta_{5}=

\displaystyle-2\mu\zeta_{4}+...,

\displaystyle\textit{þ}\zeta_{4}-\textit{ð}^{\prime}\zeta_{4}=

\displaystyle-\mu\zeta_{1}+...

and leads to the following energy estimate:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{v}\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}||\zeta_{4}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u,v^{\prime}})}+\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{a}{|u^{\prime}|^{4}}||\zeta_{5}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},v})}\lesssim\int_{0}^{v}\frac{1}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}}||\zeta_{4}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u_{\infty},v^{\prime}})}+\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{a}{|u^{\prime}|^{4}}||\zeta_{5}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},0})}

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{v}\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{a}{|u^{\prime}|^{4}}||\zeta_{4}||_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}})}||\zeta_{4}\mu||_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}})}+2\int_{0}^{v}\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{a}{|u^{\prime}|^{4}}||\zeta_{5}||_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}})}||\zeta_{1}\mu||_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}})}+...

in the scale-invariant norm. This means that
\zeta_{4}
and
\zeta_{5}
decay slower
and borderline in the scale-invariant sense, i.e. we have

\displaystyle|\zeta_{4}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{2}},\quad|\zeta_{5}|\lesssim\frac{a}{|u|^{3}},\quad\Rightarrow\quad||\{\zeta_{4},\zeta_{5}\}||_{L^{\infty}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u,v})}\sim\frac{|u|}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

More discussions can be found in Prop.
24
.
Hence terms
\zeta_{4}\bar{\zeta}_{4}
and
\zeta_{5}\bar{\phi}_{0}\mu
in the Bianchi identities for pair

(\Psi_{2},{\tilde{\Psi}}_{3})
and
({\tilde{\Psi}}_{3},\Psi_{4})
produce logarithmic growth
and obstruct scale-invariant closure under the bootstrap.

To suppress the borderline accumulation at each order,
we introduce the renormalized spinorial derivatives
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}
and
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}\equiv\zeta_{4}+\mu\phi_{0},\quad{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}\equiv\zeta_{5}+2\mu\phi_{1}.

On the one hand, from their equations

\displaystyle\textit{þ}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}-\textit{ð}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{5}

\displaystyle=-3\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}\,\mu-2\,\phi_{1}\,\bigl(\textit{ð}\mu\bigr)+...,

\displaystyle\textit{þ}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{5}-\textit{ð}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}

\displaystyle=2\Psi_{2}\,\phi_{1}+2\,\phi_{1}\,\bigl(\textit{ð}\pi\bigr)-\phi_{0}\,\bigl(\textit{ð}^{\prime}\mu\bigr)\,+...

one can keep the signature bookkeeping compatible with scale-invariant norms
and obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{v}||{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u,v^{\prime}})}+\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{a}{|u^{\prime}|^{2}}||{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},v})}\lesssim\int_{0}^{v}||{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u_{\infty},v^{\prime}})}+\int_{u_{\infty}}^{u}\frac{a}{|u^{\prime}|^{2}}||{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}||^{2}_{L^{2}_{sc}(\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},0})}+...

which show that
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}
and
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}
are normal terms in the scale-invariant norm
and lead to a sharper control of
\zeta_{4}
and
\zeta_{5}
; see Prop.
25
.
On the other hand, these new variables absorb the problematic couplings in the Bianchi identities.

The evolution equations for
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}
and
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}
contain curvature
and higher-order derivatives of connection coefficients,
so we do not attempt full top-order closure for these variables.
Instead, we close them at the next-to-top order, uniformly in the hierarchy.
Terms
\phi_{j}\textit{ð}\zeta_{4}
and
\phi_{j}\textit{ð}\zeta_{5}
in the Bianchi analysis cause
no logarithmic accumulation even if
\zeta_{4}
,
\zeta_{5}

themselves are not controlled sharply at the very top order.
Based on that observation one can then close the bootstrap without derivative loss.

1.4
Outline of the article

In Section
2
, we introduce the geometric setting, coordinate choice
and equations in T-weight formalism.
In Section
3
, we present the strategy of the proof and the basic estimates
for the frame components.
In Section
4
, we estimate the
L^{2}(\mathcal{S})
norm for non-top-order connections,

\phi_{A}
,
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
and curvatures.
The analysis of top-order derivative of connections are shown in Section
5
.
Section
6
discusses the energy estimates of
\phi_{A}
,
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
and curvatures.
Section
7
discusses the formation of trapped surfaces.

2
Geometric setup and Equations in T-weight formalism

2.1
Basic geometric setting

Let
(\mathcal{M},\bm{g})
be a 4-dimensional manifold with boundary:
outgoing null edge
\mathcal{N}_{\star}
and ingoing null edge
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\star}
.
The intersection is denoted by
\mathcal{S}_{\star}=\mathcal{N}_{\star}\cap\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\star}
.
We assume the existence of the double null foliation
(u,v)
in the future
of
\mathcal{N}_{\star}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\star}
,
where the level sets
u
-surfaces
\mathcal{N}_{u}
are outgoing null hypersurfaces and

\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{v}
represent the ingoing null hypersurfaces
with
\mathcal{N}_{0}=\mathcal{N}_{\star}
and

\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{0}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\star}
.
Define the region
\mathcal{D}_{u,v}
via

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{u,v}\equiv\bigcup_{0\leq v^{\prime}\leq v,u_{\infty}\leq u^{\prime}\leq u}\mathcal{S}_{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}}.

(8)

Denote
\mathcal{S}_{u,v}=\mathcal{N}_{u}\cap\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{v}
be the
spacelike topological 2-sphere.
We also denote
\mathcal{N}_{u}(v_{1},v_{2})
be the part of the hypersurface
\mathcal{N}_{u}

with
v_{1}\leq v\leq v_{2}
. Likewise
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{v}(u_{1},u_{2})
has a similar
definition.
One then adopt the same coordinate choice in
[
15
]
and
construct a Newman-Penrose frame
\{{\bm{l}},{\bm{n}},\bm{m},\bar{\bm{m}}\}
of the form

\displaystyle{\bm{l}}=Q{\bm{\partial}}_{v},\qquad{\bm{n}}={\bm{\partial}}_{u}+C^{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\partial}}_{\mathcal{A}},\qquad{\bm{m}}=P^{\mathcal{A}}{\bm{\partial}}_{\mathcal{A}},

(9)

where
C^{\mathcal{A}}=0
on
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\star}
, and
Q=1
on
\mathcal{N}_{\star}
.
Then we have the following properties for connection coefficients

\displaystyle\kappa=\nu=\epsilon=0,

(10a)

\displaystyle\rho=\bar{\rho},\ \ \mu=\bar{\mu},

(10b)

\displaystyle\tau=\bar{\alpha}+\beta

(10c)

in the neighbourhood of
\mathcal{D}_{u,v}
and, furthermore, with

\displaystyle\gamma-\bar{\gamma}=0\ \ \ \textrm{on}\ \ \ \mathcal{D}_{u,v}\cap\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\star}.

Moreover ons has the following equations for the frame coefficient
Q
,
P^{\mathcal{A}}

and
C^{\mathcal{A}}
:

\displaystyle DC^{\mathcal{A}}=(\bar{\tau}+\pi)P^{\mathcal{A}}+(\tau+\bar{\pi})\bar{P}^{\mathcal{A}},

(11a)

\displaystyle DP^{\mathcal{A}}=\bar{\rho}P^{\mathcal{A}}+\sigma\bar{P}^{\mathcal{A}},

(11b)

\displaystyle\Delta P^{\mathcal{A}}-\delta C^{\mathcal{A}}=-(\mu-\gamma+\bar{\gamma})P^{\mathcal{A}}-\bar{\lambda}\bar{P}^{\mathcal{A}},

(11c)

\displaystyle\Delta Q=(\gamma+\bar{\gamma})Q,

(11d)

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}P^{\mathcal{A}}-\delta\bar{P}^{\mathcal{A}}=(\alpha-\bar{\beta})P^{\mathcal{A}}-(\bar{\alpha}-\beta)\bar{P}^{\mathcal{A}},

(11e)

\displaystyle\delta Q=(\tau-\bar{\pi})Q.

(11f)

2.2
T-weight formalism and equations

In this section we introduce the equations in the T-weight formalism
which are designed to fit the PDE analysis.
Based on the GHP formalism, we assign each quantity
f
a so called T-weight
s(f)

and introduce four new differential operators
ð
,

\textit{ð}^{\prime}
,
þ
and
\textit{þ}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\textit{ð}f

\displaystyle\equiv\delta f+s(\beta-\bar{\alpha})f,\quad\textit{ð}^{\prime}f\equiv\bar{\delta}f-s(\bar{\beta}-\alpha)f,\quad\textit{þ}f\equiv Df+s(\epsilon-\bar{\epsilon})f,\quad\textit{þ}^{\prime}f\equiv\Delta f+s(\gamma-\bar{\gamma})f,

and obtain that the norm of such derivative of T-weight quantities
is independent of the spherical coordinates choice; see
[
15
]
.
Such quantity
f
is related to a tensor
T(f)
on
\mathcal{S}
and one has that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}^{k}f|^{2}\equiv\sum_{\alpha}|\mathcal{D}^{k_{i}}f|^{2}=|/\kern-5.80002pt\nabla^{k}T(f)|^{2},

where
\mathcal{D}^{k_{i}}f
is a string of order
k
of the
operators
ð
and
\textit{ð}^{\prime}
, and the sum over
\alpha
denotes all
such strings. This leads to the definition of norm on
\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle||\mathcal{D}^{k}f||^{p}_{L^{p}(\mathcal{S})}\equiv\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\mathcal{D}^{k}f|^{p},\qquad||\mathcal{D}^{k}f||_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{S})}\equiv\sup_{\mathcal{S}}|\mathcal{D}^{k}f|.

(12)

More properties can be found in
[
15
]
.

(1) Weyl spinor equations in T-weight formalism

Given a spin basis
\{o,\iota\}
, the respective NP frame is defined by

\displaystyle l^{AA^{\prime}}\equiv o^{A}\bar{o}^{A^{\prime}},\quad n^{AA^{\prime}}\equiv\iota^{A}\bar{\iota}^{A^{\prime}},\quad m^{AA^{\prime}}\equiv o^{A}\bar{\iota}^{A^{\prime}},\quad\bar{m}^{AA^{\prime}}\equiv\iota^{A}\bar{o}^{A^{\prime}}

and the connection coefficients are defined as

\displaystyle\kappa=

\displaystyle o^{A}Do_{A},\quad\epsilon=\iota^{A}Do_{A},\quad\pi=\iota^{A}D\iota_{A},\quad\tau=o^{A}\Delta o_{A},\quad\gamma=\iota^{A}\Delta o_{A},\quad\nu=\iota^{A}\Delta\iota_{A},

\displaystyle\beta=

\displaystyle\iota^{A}\delta o_{A},\quad\sigma=o^{A}\delta o_{A},\quad\mu=\iota^{A}\delta\iota_{A},\quad\alpha=\iota^{A}\bar{\delta}o_{A},\quad\rho=o^{A}\bar{\delta}o_{A},\quad\lambda=\iota^{A}\bar{\delta}\iota_{A}

where the NP derivatives are defined by

\displaystyle D=l^{AA^{\prime}}\nabla_{AA^{\prime}},\quad\Delta=n^{AA^{\prime}}\nabla_{AA^{\prime}},\quad\delta=m^{AA^{\prime}}\nabla_{AA^{\prime}},\quad\bar{\delta}=\bar{m}^{AA^{\prime}}\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}.

Define the components of
\phi^{A}
with respect to the spin basis
\{o,\iota\}
by

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\equiv\phi_{A}o^{A},\quad\phi_{1}\equiv\phi_{A}\iota^{A}.

Define the components of
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
with respect to the spin basis
\{o,\iota\}
by

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}

\displaystyle\equiv\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}o^{A}o^{B}\bar{o}^{A^{\prime}},\quad\zeta_{1}\equiv\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}o^{A}\iota^{B}\bar{o}^{A^{\prime}},\quad\zeta_{2}\equiv\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}\iota^{A}\iota^{B}\bar{o}^{A^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}

\displaystyle\equiv\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}o^{A}o^{B}\bar{\iota}^{A^{\prime}},\quad\zeta_{4}\equiv\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}o^{A}\iota^{B}\bar{\iota}^{A^{\prime}},\quad\zeta_{5}\equiv\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}\iota^{A}\iota^{B}\bar{\iota}^{A^{\prime}}.

(13)

The T-weight of such quantities are listed as follows:

\displaystyle s=-\frac{3}{2}:\quad\zeta_{3}

\displaystyle s=-\frac{1}{2}:\quad\phi_{0},\quad\zeta_{0},\quad\zeta_{4},

\displaystyle s=\frac{1}{2}:\quad\phi_{1},\quad\zeta_{1},\quad\zeta_{5},

\displaystyle s=\frac{3}{2}:\quad\zeta_{2},

We obtain the T-weight equations for
\nabla_{AA^{\prime}}\phi^{A}=0
:

\displaystyle\textit{þ}\,\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}\,\pi+\phi_{1}\,\rho-\frac{\phi_{0}\,\bar{\tau}}{2}+\textit{ð}^{\prime}\,\phi_{0},

(14a)

\displaystyle\textit{þ}^{\prime}\,\phi_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega\,\phi_{0}}{2}-\phi_{0}\,\mu-\frac{\phi_{1}\,\tau}{2}+\textit{ð}\,\phi_{1}.

(14b)

The equations of the definition of
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
(
13
) are shown in appendix.
A.1
.
The equations for
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
, i.e. (
5
) are presented in appendix
A.2
.
The schematic form are

\displaystyle\{\textit{þ},\textit{þ}^{\prime}\}\zeta-\textit{ð}\zeta=\Psi\phi+\Gamma\zeta+\zeta\phi^{2},

\displaystyle\textit{ð}^{\prime}\zeta-\textit{ð}\zeta=\Gamma\zeta+\zeta\phi^{2}+\Psi\phi,

where
\zeta
denote the components of
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
,
\phi
denote the components of
\phi_{A}
,

\Gamma
denote the connection coefficients and
\Psi
denote the curvatures.
As we have already shown in
[
26
]
, such equations can be
divided into two groups, one group does not contain Weyl curvature
A.2.1
,
and another group does contain; see
A.2.2
.

(2) The Einstein field equation

Expand the Einstein field equation (
7
) with the fields
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
,
one obtains the following

\displaystyle\Phi_{00}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\bigl(\bar{\zeta}_{0}\phi_{0}-\zeta_{0}\bar{\phi}_{0}\bigr),

(15a)

\displaystyle\Phi_{01}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\bigl(2\bar{\zeta}_{1}\phi_{0}-\zeta_{3}\bar{\phi}_{0}-\zeta_{0}\bar{\phi}_{1}\bigr),

(15b)

\displaystyle\Phi_{02}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\bigl(\bar{\zeta}_{2}\phi_{0}-\zeta_{3}\bar{\phi}_{1}\bigr),

(15c)

\displaystyle\Phi_{11}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\bigl(\bar{\zeta}_{4}\phi_{0}-\zeta_{4}\bar{\phi}_{0}+\bar{\zeta}_{1}\phi_{1}-\zeta_{1}\bar{\phi}_{1}\bigr),

(15d)

\displaystyle\Phi_{12}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\bigl(\bar{\zeta}_{5}\phi_{0}+\bar{\zeta}_{2}\phi_{1}-2\zeta_{4}\bar{\phi}_{1}\bigr),

(15e)

\displaystyle\Phi_{22}

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\bigl(\bar{\zeta}_{5}\phi_{1}-\zeta_{5}\bar{\phi}_{1}\bigr),

(15f)

\displaystyle\Lambda

\displaystyle=0.

(15g)

(3) The structure equations, Bianchi identities and the renormalised Weyl curvature

The structure equations have the schematic form

\displaystyle\{\textit{þ},\textit{þ}^{\prime}\}\Gamma-\textit{ð}\Gamma=\zeta\phi+\Gamma\Gamma+\Psi.

The fully explicit expressions can be found in the appendix
A.3
.
For the Bianchi identities, we introduce the following two
renormalised Weyl curvature components which are defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Psi}_{1}\equiv\Psi_{1}-\Phi_{01},\quad\tilde{\Psi}_{3}\equiv\Psi_{3}-\Phi_{21},

(16a)

with which one can formulate a Hodge system.
The Bianchi Identities have the schematic form:

\displaystyle\{\textit{þ},\textit{þ}^{\prime}\}\Psi_{i}-\{\textit{ð},\textit{ð}^{\prime}\}\Psi_{j}=

\displaystyle+\zeta\phi\Gamma+\phi\textit{ð}\zeta+\Psi\phi^{2}+\zeta\phi^{3}+\zeta^{2}+\Gamma\Psi_{k}.

The fully explicit equations are shown in
A.4
.

2.3
Further ancillary fields

Note that we prescribe the Minkowski data on
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{\star}
on which the two null expansions are

\displaystyle\theta_{{\bm{l}}}(u,v=0)=-\rho-\bar{\rho}=-2\rho=-2\cdot\frac{1}{u-v}=-\frac{2}{u};

\displaystyle\theta_{{\bm{n}}}(u,v=0)=\mu+\bar{\mu}=2\mu=2\cdot\frac{1}{u-v}=\frac{2}{u},

we anticipate that, in
\mathcal{D}_{u,v}
, the ingoing expansion
\mu
change slightly,
hence it is convenient do define the following new quantity

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}\equiv\mu-\frac{1}{u}.

(17)

whose transport equations are

\displaystyle\textit{þ}\tilde{\mu}-\textit{ð}\pi-\rho\tilde{\mu}=-\rho\tilde{\mu}+\mu\rho+\Psi_{2}+\lambda\sigma+\pi\bar{\pi},

(18a)

\displaystyle\textit{þ}^{\prime}\tilde{\mu}+2\mu\tilde{\mu}=\tilde{\mu}^{2}-{\underline{\omega}}\mu-\lambda\bar{\lambda}-2\mathrm{i}\bigl(\bar{\zeta}_{5}\phi_{1}-\zeta_{5}\bar{\phi}_{1}\bigr).

(18b)

Another quantity is related to the derivative of frame coefficient
Q
, we define

\displaystyle{\underline{\varrho}}\equiv\textit{þ}\log Q

(19)

to track the property of
Q
as we did in
[
15
]
.
The equation for
{\underline{\varrho}}
is

\displaystyle\textit{þ}^{\prime}{\underline{\varrho}}=\Psi_{2}+\bar{\Psi}_{2}+{\underline{\varrho}}\,{\underline{\omega}}+2\,\mathrm{i}\,\bar{\zeta}_{4}\,\phi_{0}-2\,\mathrm{i}\,\zeta_{4}\,\bar{\phi}_{0}+2\,\mathrm{i}\,\bar{\zeta}_{1}\,\phi_{1}-2\,\mathrm{i}\,\zeta_{1}\,\bar{\phi}_{1}+2\,\pi\,\bar{\pi}+2\,\pi\,\tau+2\,\bar{\pi}\,\bar{\tau}.

(20)

In order to eliminate the logarithmic contribution
from terms containing
\zeta_{4}
and
\zeta_{5}
in the energy estimate
for Bianchi identities, we define the following renormalized quantities to absorb such terms:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}\equiv\zeta_{4}+\mu\phi_{0},\quad{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}\equiv\zeta_{5}+2\mu\phi_{1}.

(21)

Making use of the Equation of motion for
\phi_{A}
and the structure equation of
\mu
,
one can then obtain the equations for pair
({\tilde{\zeta}}_{4},{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5})
:

\displaystyle\textit{þ}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}-\textit{ð}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{5}

\displaystyle=-\,\mathrm{i}\,\bar{\zeta}_{2}\,\phi_{1}^{2}-\frac{{\underline{\omega}}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}}{2}+2\,\mathrm{i}\,\phi_{1}\,\bar{\phi}_{1}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}-\mathrm{i}\,\phi_{0}\,\phi_{1}\,\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}-\zeta_{2}\,\bar{\lambda}-\phi_{0}\,\lambda\,\bar{\lambda}-3\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}\,\mu

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{5}\,\tau}{2}-\phi_{1}\,\mu\,\tau-2\,\phi_{1}\,\bigl(\textit{ð}\mu\bigr)\,,

(22a)

\displaystyle\textit{þ}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{5}-\textit{ð}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}

\displaystyle=2\Psi_{2}\,\phi_{1}+\mathrm{i}\,\bar{\zeta}_{1}\,\phi_{1}^{2}+\mathrm{i}\,\zeta_{2}\,\phi_{0}\,\bar{\phi}_{1}-\mathrm{i}\,\zeta_{1}\,\phi_{1}\,\bar{\phi}_{1}-\mathrm{i}\,\bar{\phi}_{0}\,\phi_{1}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}-\mathrm{i}\,\phi_{0}\,\phi_{1}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{i}\,\phi_{0}\,\bar{\phi}_{0}\,\tilde{\zeta}_{5}-\zeta_{3}\,\lambda+2\,\tilde{\zeta}_{4}\,\pi+\zeta_{2}\,\bar{\pi}+2\,\phi_{1}\,\pi\,\bar{\pi}+\tilde{\zeta}_{5}\,\rho+\phi_{1}\,\mu\,\rho

\displaystyle\quad+2\,\phi_{1}\,\lambda\,\sigma+\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{4}\,\bar{\tau}}{2}-\phi_{0}\,\mu\,\tau+2\,\phi_{1}\,\bigl(\textit{ð}\pi\bigr)-\phi_{0}\,\bigl(\textit{ð}^{\prime}\mu\bigr)\,.

(22b)

3
Preliminaries

In this section we introduce the norms, strategy of proofs and essential mathematical tools.

3.1
Signatures and scale-invariant norms

Following the assumptions in
[
8
,
9
,
15
]
,
we assume that on
\mathcal{N}_{\star}
, the connection
coefficients satisfy asymptotical behaviour

\displaystyle|\rho|\lesssim\frac{1}{|u_{\infty}|},\quad|\sigma|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|},\quad|\mu|\lesssim\frac{1}{|u_{\infty}|},\quad|{\tilde{\mu}}|=|\mu+\frac{1}{|u_{\infty}|}|\lesssim\frac{1}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},\quad|\lambda|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},

\displaystyle|{\underline{\omega}}|\lesssim\frac{a}{|u_{\infty}|^{3}},\quad|\tau|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},\quad|\pi|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},

and that for the components of the Weyl tensor we assume

\displaystyle|\Psi_{0}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|},\quad|\Psi_{1}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},\quad|\Psi_{2}|\lesssim\frac{a}{|u_{\infty}|^{3}},\quad|\Psi_{3}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{4}},\quad|\Psi_{4}|\lesssim\frac{a^{2}}{|u_{\infty}|^{5}}.

For the spinor components we assume

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|},\quad|\phi_{1}|\lesssim\frac{a}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},

and for the components of
\zeta_{ABA^{\prime}}
we assume

\displaystyle|\zeta_{0}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|},\quad|\zeta_{1}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},\quad|\zeta_{2}|\lesssim\frac{a}{|u_{\infty}|^{3}},

\displaystyle|\zeta_{3}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},\quad|\zeta_{4}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},\quad|\zeta_{5}|\lesssim\frac{a}{|u_{\infty}|^{3}},

From the definition of the renormalised Weyl curvature
{\tilde{\Psi}}_{1}
and
{\tilde{\Psi}}_{3}
, we then have

\displaystyle|{\tilde{\Psi}}_{1}|=

\displaystyle|\Psi_{1}-2\mathrm{i}\bar{\zeta}_{1}\phi_{0}+\mathrm{i}\zeta_{3}\bar{\phi}_{0}+\mathrm{i}\zeta_{0}\bar{\phi}_{1}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{2}},

\displaystyle|{\tilde{\Psi}}_{3}|=

\displaystyle|\Psi_{3}-2\mathrm{i}\bar{\zeta}_{4}\phi_{1}+\mathrm{i}\zeta_{5}\bar{\phi}_{0}-\mathrm{i}\bar{\zeta}_{0}\phi_{1}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{4}}

which show that the renormalised Weyl curvature have the same behaviour as
\Psi_{1}
and
\Psi_{3}
.

For the renormalized
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}
and
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}
we assume

\displaystyle{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}\equiv\zeta_{4}+\mu\phi_{0},\quad{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}\equiv\zeta_{5}+2\mu\phi_{1}.

We assume

\displaystyle|{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}|\lesssim\frac{a}{|u_{\infty}|^{3}},\quad|{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}|\lesssim\frac{a^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|u_{\infty}|^{4}}.

Remark 2
.

A single left–handed Weyl spinor admits no nontrivial spherical symmetry;
nevertheless, our data produce a trapped
2
-sphere that is quantifiably close to round.
This can be obtained by the estimate of the Gaussian curvature
K
at the Minkowskian level.
In the existence theorem we show that
K\sim|u|^{-2}
in
L^{\infty}_{sc}
and
L^{2}_{sc}
,
and obtain uniform bounds on
\mathcal{D}^{i}K
; see Prop.
13
and Remark
4
.
Consequently, the terminal trapped sphere deviates from a round metric by at most
O(a^{-\frac{1}{4}})

in the scale-invariant norms.

This near-sphericity reflects the low-angular-momentum character of the initial flux:
in Newman–Penrose variables,
\Phi_{00}=T_{ab}l^{a}l^{b}
measures outgoing null energy density,
while
\Phi_{01}=T_{ab}l^{a}m^{b}
encodes tangential momentum/rotation flux.
In our model these read

\displaystyle\Phi_{00}=2\mathrm{i}\bigl(\bar{\zeta}_{0}\phi_{0}-\zeta_{0}\bar{\phi}_{0}\bigr),\quad\Phi_{01}=\mathrm{i}\bigl(2\bar{\zeta}_{1}\phi_{0}-\zeta_{3}\bar{\phi}_{0}-\zeta_{0}\bar{\phi}_{1}\bigr),

and the initial data satisfies
\|\Phi_{01}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S})}\ll\|\Phi_{00}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S})}
,
our existence theorem shows that such relation is maintained in the working region
and hence amounts to a low-angular momentum regime with predominantly radial spinor flux,
see Remark
7
.
This bias drives focusing without appreciable twist, consistent with the Gaussian curvature control
and the formation of an almost round trapped surface.

Follow the basic design in An’s work
[
8
]
,
we introduce the signature for decay rates to each quantity in the following

\Psi_{0}

\Psi_{1}

\Psi_{2}

\Psi_{3}

\Psi_{4}

\omega

\vartheta

\tau

\pi

\mu,\lambda,\tilde{\mu}

{\underline{\omega}}

{\underline{\chi}}

s_{2}

0

1/2

1

3/2

2

0

0

1/2

1/2

1/2

\phi_{0},\zeta_{0}

\phi_{1}
,
\zeta_{1}
,
\zeta_{3}

\zeta_{2}
,
\zeta_{4}
,
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{4}

\zeta_{5}
,
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{5}

s_{2}

1/2
